Seventeen years have passed since the Children's Rights Convention came into effect for Turkey in 1995. The final process was almost as if the government had no idea about the contract, and the children were no more than a great disappointment. In Turkey, the basic rights guaranteed in the contract, including children's rights, are either violated by the state or by the lack of necessary preventive mechanisms. Suicide and suicide attempts are a frightening increase in the suicide rates of children who are up to 15 years old. A total of 869 children were killed in violent acts of self-inflicted violence that resulted in death between 2000 and 2010. Child suicides reached 76 in 2008, 95 in 2009 and 107 in 2010. This number was 57 in 2000 and has increased almost twice in ten years. Given the fact that there have been about 10 suicide attempts in exchange for each suicide, it's more understandable how serious the problem is. In the same period, suicides between the ages of 15 and 19 appear to be more serious. In this age group, a total of 8,223 people committed suicide over the course of 11 years. Research results and relevant expert comments also show that child suicides can be avoided by taking necessary measures, but they are increasing by the day. In accordance with the relevant provisions of the Children's Rights Convention, the situation will only get worse if the child's intellectual transformations are not provided, especially in terms of education, social services and the principle of the child's higher benefits. Clearly, the development of processes in which children can express themselves freely in a healthy and secure social environment and reveal their potential is the responsibility of the state's relevant institutions under the Convention on Child Rights. Among the most common right violations are the most important issues of violations under the right to protect the child, including the deaths and injuries caused by explosives, and the injuries caused during social actions. In this context, it is clear that the state is obliged to create all physical, social, economic and legal processes with a perspective that focuss on the child's well-being. It is useful to set up a research commission to conduct necessary research on this subject and to take precautions. [[Mardin]] [[Lug]] [[Bingöl]] [[Mwah.]] [[Adana]] [[Pain]] 7) Ayla Akat Ata [[Batman]] [[Şırnak]] [[Bitlis]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Hakkari]] [[Hakkari]] 15) The Leader of the Secret [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[Kars]] [[Mersin]] [[Mwah.]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Van]] [[Van]] Turkey signed the Children's Rights Act signed by the UN General Assembly on 14 September 1990. The contract guarantees the benefit of children of many basic rights, including living, development, protection, participation, social and cultural rights, freedom of expression and organisation. Although this signed contract was implemented in 1995, children faced rights violations, and the practices that were tried by the state were no more than disappointing. Article 6 of the Children's Rights Convention states that each child has the basic right to live, that the state must make the maximum effort possible for the child's survival and development. In Turkey, the state's maximum effort, which is expected to demonstrate, is more likely to result in rights violations of children. In June 2012, the IHD Children's Rights Commission stated that at least 552 children have been killed by state forces in the past 24 years, as well as the number of children killed since 2002, when the EC PARTY came to power.
